    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — struct_self_consist_ie\\n\\n> Updated 2026-05-14 with 1024-token corrected data (D054-D060) + bootstrap significance test (exp_016_rerun_1024_bootstrap). All values from exp_012_rerun_1024 / exp_016_rerun_1024. Previous D019 values (max_tokens=512) were systematically biased by truncation artifacts.\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (via ARR May 2026)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May cycle, 2026-05-25\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 条)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (Positive — Quality Estimation)\\nStructural consistency signals (exact-match voting, entity voting confidence, soft Jaccard) are the strongest unsupervised quality estimators for entity-level IE, significantly outperforming model confidence (log-probability) at instance-level quality prediction. EM achieves AUROC 0.749 on NER (best single signal); voting_conf leads in rank correlation (ρ=0.379). All structural consistency signals substantially outperform logprob (AUROC 0.578, ρ=0.205).\\n\\n**Key insight**: The contribution is about a FAMILY of structural consistency signals for QUALITY ESTIMATION (predicting which instances are reliable), not for per-sample selection. EM, voting_conf, and SJ all capture entity-across-sample consistency from different angles. EM is strongest for binary discrimination (AUROC), voting_conf for rank ordering (ρ), SJ for generalizability to arbitrary structured outputs.\\n\\n**Evidence** (SciERC, N=8, T=1.0, exp_012_rerun_1024):\\n- NER full (n=529): ρ — voting_conf 0.379, SJ 0.360, EM 0.295, FK 0.267, logprob 0.205; AUROC — EM 0.749, SJ 0.692, voting_conf 0.684, FK 0.629, logprob 0.578; τ — 待补\\n- NER cond (n=477): AUROC — EM 0.756, SJ 0.677, voting_conf 0.662, FK 0.608, logprob 0.563; ρ — 待补\\n- RE full (n=397): AUROC — EM 0.706, voting_conf 0.694, SJ 0.670, FK 0.609, logprob 0.601; ρ — 待补\\n- RE cond (n=238): AUROC — EM 0.752, SJ 0.646, voting_conf 0.642, FK 0.551, logprob 0.519; ρ — 待补\\n- Temperature robustness: SJ Δρ=±0.01 across T=0.7/1.0; FK Δρ=±0.06-0.09 (SJ far more stable)\\n- ECE (NER): EM 0.353, voting 0.381, SJ 0.453, FK 0.451, logprob 0.706\\n- ECE (RE): voting 0.272, EM 0.355, SJ 0.387, FK 0.441, logprob 0.769\\n\\n### Claim 2 (Novel Finding — Correlation-Selection Gap)\\nInstance-level quality estimation ≠ per-sample selection ability. Despite strong instance-level discrimination (AUROC up to 0.749), NO unsupervised confidence signal significantly outperforms greedy decoding for best-of-N selection on either task. On NER, logprob shows only a marginal +0.6pp advantage over greedy, confirmed NOT significant by bootstrap (95% CI [−0.030, +0.042], permutation p=0.39, Cohen's d=0.038, sign test win rate=0.466). On RE, greedy outperforms ALL signals. The oracle headroom is large (NER +13pp, RE +18pp) but current signals cannot exploit it.\\n\\n**exp_016_rerun_1024 evidence** (SciERC, N=8, T=1.0):\\n- NER best-of-N (n=529): logprob 0.6501 > sj 0.6450 = ensemble 0.6450 > greedy 0.6439 > voting 0.6337 > random 0.6077 (oracle 0.7758)\\n- RE best-of-N (n=397): greedy 0.4042 > logprob 0.3948 > sj 0.3855 = ensemble 0.3855 > voting 0.3592 > random 0.3544 (oracle 0.5850)\\n- Ensemble AUROC (5-fold CV LR): NER 0.666 ± 0.070, RE 0.662 ± 0.078\\n\\n**exp_016_rerun_1024_bootstrap evidence** (3-seed bootstrap, 1000 iterations):\\n- NER logprob vs greedy: bootstrap 95% CI [−0.030, +0.042], permutation p=0.39\\n- Effect size: Cohen's d=0.038 (negligible), sign test win rate=0.466\\n- Conclusion: No signal significantly outperforms greedy on either task\\n\\n**Key insight**: Consistency signals are INSTANCE-LEVEL aggregates (one score per instance). They predict which inputs are easy/hard, NOT which sample is best. The correlation-selection gap is confirmed with statistical rigor: on NER, logprob's +0.6pp advantage is not significant (p=0.39); on RE, greedy outperforms all signals. This strengthens the \\\"Weak Selectors\\\" narrative — the gap is not a marginal call but a clear null result.\\n\\n**Implications**: (a) For selective prediction / abstention → use structural consistency (AUROC 0.75). (b) For best-of-N selection → no signal reliably helps; the marginal differences are noise. (c) The gap between oracle and best signal is the paper's most important finding: the information exists in the sample set but current unsupervised signals cannot access it.\\n\\n### Claim 3 (Diagnostic — task-complexity boundary)\\nThe effectiveness of consistency-based confidence is governed by task structural complexity: compound error propagation in relational tasks degrades the consistency-accuracy correlation on the full instance set, but the signal remains valid within the model's capability range. This boundary applies across all confidence signals tested.\\n\\n**MVP evidence**: RE full vs cond AUROC lift — EM: 0.706→0.752 (+4.6pp), SJ: 0.670→0.646 (−2.4pp, 待确认), voting_conf: 0.694→0.642 (−5.2pp, 待确认). Component decomposition shows all RE components equally weak (head_sj rho=0.210). T=0.7 naturally separates knowledge-gap vs uncertainty errors.\\n\\n**exp-005 evidence**: Conditional filtering dwarfs temperature tuning — RE SJ cond lift +0.07~0.14 vs temperature effect ±0.01. T=1.0 preserves more oracle headroom (RE: 0.167 vs 0.131) and diversity (1-SJ: 0.389 vs 0.313). Recommendation: T=1.0 + conditional filtering, not temperature optimization.\\n\\n**exp-001 N-scaling evidence** (SciERC, n=200 subset, ⚠️ needs full-set confirmation):\\n- RE N=16: voting_conf ρ=0.448 (vs N=8 0.340, +0.108), SJ ρ=0.333 (vs 0.244, +0.089), all signals improve +0.07~0.10\\n- NER N=16: flat (already saturated at N=8) — voting_conf 0.401 vs 0.392\\n- **Key insight**: The task-complexity boundary is partially a SAMPLE BUDGET boundary. Compound error propagation increases per-instance variance, requiring more samples (N=16 vs N=8) to average out noise. This makes Claim 2 more nuanced: RE consistency fails at N=8 not because the signal is absent, but because it's noisier and needs more samples to manifest.\\n- ⚠️ Caveat: exp-001 ran on 200/529 instances. Full-set replication needed before claiming in paper.\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: LLM-based structured prediction (IE) increasingly uses multi-sample generation, but practitioners lack guidance on which confidence signals to trust — and for what purpose. Crucially, it is unknown whether signals that predict output quality (instance-level) also enable effective sample selection (per-sample-level).\\n\\n**Existing approaches & gaps**: Self-consistency in reasoning uses exact-match voting (Wang et al. 2023). USC (Chen et al. 2024) uses LLM-as-judge. For structured prediction, no systematic comparison exists across confidence signal families. More importantly, prior work conflates quality estimation with selection — assuming that high correlation with accuracy translates to effective best-of-N selection.\\n\\n**Our framing**: We systematically compare five confidence signals across four families for IE: (1) model confidence (log-probability), (2) surface agreement (FK, exact-match), (3) set-aware structural similarity (SJ), (4) entity-level majority voting. We make three findings:\\n1. Structural consistency (EM/voting_conf/SJ) provides the strongest quality estimation (EM AUROC 0.749, voting_conf ρ=0.379 vs logprob AUROC 0.578, ρ=0.205)\\n2. However, a **correlation-selection gap** exists: despite strong instance-level discrimination, NO signal significantly outperforms greedy decoding for best-of-N selection on either task (NER logprob +0.6pp, bootstrap p=0.39; RE greedy > all signals)\\n3. All signals degrade on relational tasks due to compound error propagation\\n\\n**Headline result template**: \\\"On NER, exact-match voting achieves AUROC 0.749 for quality estimation, and voting confidence leads rank correlation (ρ=0.379) — both substantially outperforming log-probability (AUROC 0.578, ρ=0.205). However, this quality estimation capability does NOT translate to effective best-of-N selection — no signal significantly outperforms greedy decoding on either task (bootstrap 95% CI [−0.030, +0.042], p=0.39; oracle headroom: NER +13pp, RE +18pp). We identify a fundamental correlation-selection gap: instance-level consistency predicts WHICH INPUTS are reliable, not WHICH OUTPUTS are best.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### R1: \\\"Only one NER dataset — generalization unclear\\\"\\n- **Mitigation**: 2 datasets (SciERC + CoNLL-2003) × 2 models (Qwen3 + LLaMA) = 4 NER configurations. exp_017_llama_conll_infer SJ ρ=0.431 matches Qwen CoNLL ρ=0.436, demonstrating cross-model × cross-dataset consistency. Limitation: informal text domains (social media etc.) not covered — acknowledge in Limitations section.\\n- **Priority**: P0\\n\\n### R2: \\\"Only one model family (Qwen3-8B)\\\"\\n- **Mitigation**: exp-007 (LLaMA-3.1-8B) shows model-agnostic effect\\n- **Priority**: P0\\n\\n### R3: \\\"FK is a strawman baseline — what about log-prob, USC, exact-match?\\\"\\n- **Mitigation**: exp-012 (log-prob), exp-013 (exact-match), exp-014 (entity voting) provide comprehensive baseline comparison. Results: structural consistency family (EM+SJ+voting) >> log-prob >> FK. USC discussed in related work (LLM-as-judge, orthogonal approach).\\n- **Priority**: P0, RESOLVED — results decisive\\n\\n### R3b: \\\"EM beats voting_conf and SJ on AUROC — why present SJ/voting_conf?\\\"\\n- **Mitigation**: EM is a strict binary signal (all-or-nothing match); SJ captures partial agreement and generalizes to arbitrary structured outputs (relations, events). Voting_conf provides granular entity-level scores. NER full AUROC: EM 0.749 > SJ 0.692 > voting_conf 0.684, but ρ: voting_conf 0.379 > SJ 0.360 > EM 0.295. EM excels at binary discrimination but underperforms on rank ordering. SJ is also more temperature-robust (Δρ=±0.01).
